Then I look back at the screen and realize I don't even know what I just looked at.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things that happened a long time ago?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My long-term memory is actually pretty good. I can remember things from years ago just fine, it's just the day-to-day stuff that I can't seem to hold onto.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any physical complaints, such as headaches or fatigue?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have any physical complaints like that. I don't get headaches often, and I wouldn't say I'm unusually tired or anything. Physically, I feel fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your personality?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I'm still the same person I've always been, just... struggling a bit more with keeping my focus straight. But my personality hasn't changed.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble learning new procedures or tasks at work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I do maintenance work, so I'm pretty used to figuring things out and working with my hands. If someone shows me a new way to fix something or explains a new procedure, I understand it just fine. Actually learning the task isn't the problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel confused or disoriented at work?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I know where I am and what's going on around me. I don't get mixed up or lost like that. I mean, it's really just... keeping my mind on the actual task that's the hard part, but I'm never actually confused.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things like appointments, birthdays, or anniversaries outside of work?\n\nPatient: Well, it happens outside of work, sure, but... honestly, work is the main reason I'm sitting here right now. What actually brought me in is that I keep forgetting scheduled callbacks, work orders, and even just basic follow-ups I promised people on the job. It’s all piled up so much lately that work actually sent me here. They basically told me I need to get this sorted out before I lose my shot at moving up.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering how to do tasks that you've done many times before at work?\n\nPatient: No, I don't forget how to do the actual work. Like I said, I'm good with my hands. If I've fixed a boiler or a leaky pipe a hundred times, I know exactly how to do it. It's just... remembering that I'm supposed to go fix it in the first place, or remembering to call the tenant back to say it's done. The actual steps of the job, though? No, I don't forget those.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant life events or stressors outside of work recently?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Things at home are pretty steady. There hasn't been any major changes, no moves, no family emergencies or anything like that. It's really just the work stuff that's been piling up and causing me grief.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your memory problems at work might be related to something going on in your personal life, even if you don't think anything significant is happening?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no. I mean, I get why you're asking, but my personal life is fine. There's no hidden stress or drama at home spilling over into my job. It's just... I just can't seem to keep the work orders and callbacks straight in my head. I wish I could say it was because I'm distracted by something going on at home, but that's just not it. It's just me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering tasks or instructions when they are not related to work or any source of stress?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, if my husband asks me to pick up milk on the way home, I might forget it sometimes, but it's not like the work stuff. The work stuff is just... constant. At home, I can usually keep track of what needs doing around the house or running errands. It's really just when I'm on the clock that everything seems to slip through the cracks.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

So, that's what brings me in.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself forgetting things outside of work as well?\n\nPatient: Honestly, not really. I mean, I haven't noticed it being an issue at home. It's mostly just... well, work is where everything is falling apart. At home, I don't really have a bunch of schedules or work orders to keep straight, so if I do forget little things, it hasn't caused any real problems. So, no, I wouldn't say it happens much outside of my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or stressed about your job performance or the potential consequences your boss mentioned?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't really call it anxiety. I mean, I know it's a serious problem, and I obviously don't want to lose my job or miss out on the promotion. But I'm not sitting around stressing about it all the time. It's more just... frustrating, I guess? I just know I need to figure out why I keep dropping the ball and get it fixed so my boss is happy.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood lately?\n\nPatient: No, my mood has been pretty normal. I don't feel down or depressed or anything like that. Just annoyed with myself for messing up at work, but my actual mood is fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a head injury?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I've never hit my head or had a concussion or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty concentrating on tasks at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really do. Like, I can pull up a digital work order, thinking I’ve got a handle on it. But halfway through reading the notes, my mind just... slips off. I'll get distracted by a noise in the building, or I start thinking about the next job I have to do. Then I look back at the screen and realize I don't even know what I just looked at.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things that happened a long time ago?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My long-term memory is actually pretty good. I can remember things from years ago just fine, it's just the day-to-day stuff that I can't seem to hold onto.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any physical complaints, such as headaches or fatigue?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have any physical complaints like that. I don't get headaches often, and I wouldn't say I'm unusually tired or anything. Physically, I feel fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your personality?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I'm still the same person I've always been, just... struggling a bit more with keeping my focus straight. But my personality hasn't changed.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble learning new procedures or tasks at work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I do maintenance work, so I'm pretty used to figuring things out and working with my hands. If someone shows me a new way to fix something or explains a new procedure, I understand it just fine. Actually learning the task isn't the problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel confused or disoriented at work?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I know where I am and what's going on around me. I don't get mixed up or lost like that. I mean, it's really just... keeping my mind on the actual task that's the hard part, but I'm never actually confused.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things like appointments, birthdays, or anniversaries outside of work?\n\nPatient: Well, it happens outside of work, sure, but... honestly, work is the main reason I'm sitting here right now. Like I said, I'm good with my hands. If I've fixed a boiler or a leaky pipe a hundred times, I know exactly how to do it. No, I don't forget those.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant life events or stressors outside of work recently?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Things at home are pretty steady. There hasn't been any major changes, no moves, no family emergencies or anything like that.
